[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 11694)\nTotal output lines: 5\n\n{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"8391b8","wall_time_seconds":1.0667302,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":84,"output":"Get-Content: \\r\\nLine |\\r\\n   2 |  Get-Content src/bootcamps/conditionals/pages/OpeningCourse.tsx\\r\\n     |  ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\\r\\n     | Cannot find path \'C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os-projects\\\\lsatu-conditional-bootcamp\\\\src\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\pages\\\\OpeningCourse.tsx\' because it does not exist.\\r\\n"}}\n{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"16b00b","wall_time_seconds":0.4343121,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":650,"output":"import { Children, cloneElement, Fragment, isValidElement, useEffect, useId, useRef, useState, type ReactElement, type ReactNode } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { Fundamentals, GroupLessons } from \'./ConditionalOpeningLesson\';\\r\\nimport { conditionalReferenceContent, IntegratedItem, referencePracticeItems, ReferenceQuestion, RuleDiagram, TargetReader, targetExamples, WordFamily } from \'./ConditionalReferenceSequence\';\\r\\nimport { CounterexampleLab, NegativeCaseLab } from \'./CounterexampleLab\';\\r\\nimport { OpeningMixedDrill } from \'./OpeningMixedDrill\';\\r\\nimport { PracticeTaskRunner } from \'./PracticeTaskRunner\';\\r\\nimport { StudyDeck } from \'./StudyDeck\';\\r\\nimport \'./must-arrow-note.css\';\\r\\nimport { MustArrowPractice } from \'./MustArrowPractice\';\\r\\n\\r\\ntype ContentSection = ReactElement<{ id?: string; number?: number; title?: string; children?: ReactNode; dark?: boolean; \'data-reference-boundary\'?: boolean }>;\\r\\n\\r\\n// Compose the approved source sections directly. Their stateful exercises are\\r\\n// mounted by React; the former reader-page renderer is never invoked here.\\r\\nfunction sections(children: ReactNode): ContentSection[] {\\r\\n  return Children.toArray(children).flatMap(child => {\\r\\n    if (!isValidElement<ContentSection[\'props\']>(child)) return [];\\r\\n    return child.type === Fragment ? sections(child.props.children) : [child];\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction reference(number: number): ContentSection {\\r\\n  const section = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(item => item.props.number === number);\\r\\n  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional reference section ${number}`);\\r\\n  return section;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction groupSections(group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\') {\\r\\n  return sections(GroupLessons({ id: group }).props.children);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction groupSection(group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\', title: string) {\\r\\n  const section = groupSections(group).find(item => item.props.id === title || item.props.title === title);\\r\\n  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional section: ${title}`);\\r\\n  const sectionId = `course-${group}-${title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, \'-\')}`;\\r\\n  return cloneElement(section, { key: sectionId, id: sectionId });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction NoteSection({ title, children }: { title: string; children: ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"co-section\\"><div className=\\"co-section-heading\\"><h2>{title}</h2></div>{children}</section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ReferenceBody({ number, omitTransition = false }: { number: number; omitTransition?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const source = reference(number);\\r\\n  const children = Children.toArray(source.props.children);\\r\\n"}}\n{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"18b52b","wall_time_seconds":0.4619117,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":72,"output":"rg: src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/ConditionalReference*: The filename, directory name, or volume label syntax is incorrect. (os error 123)\\nrg: src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/Fundamentals*: The filename, directory name, or volume label syntax is incorrect. (os error 123)\\n"}}\n{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"385f62","wall_time_seconds":0.8532693,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":22429,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 22429)\\nTotal output lines: 1053\\n\\n---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT teaching standard, selected 2026-10-02:** Joshua chose the supplied Conditional Reasoning transcription as the reference for how LSAT material should sound and unfold. Read [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) before drafting lessons, notes, explanations, drills, flashcards, quizzes, or instructional feedback. It contains the detailed source analysis, original calibration examples, progression, and review rubric. [Source identity and limits](sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) are preserved separately. This choice develops the teaching-content register below; correspondence and marketing retain their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Air between ideas. Real correspondence is short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters). In teaching, vary sentence and paragraph length with the reasoning: give an explanation room, then let a short sentence settle the consequence. Do not impose email brevity on a lesson or cut its intermediate reasoning.\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\"; \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs: never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Best,\\r\\n> Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Apology first, as the opening line.** \\"I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\" The ownership sits in the first two sentences, before any explanation.\\r\\n2. **State their position plainly, as a confirmation.** \\"To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet.\\" Answer the question they actually asked. Don\'t mention the package or credits unless the situation calls for it.\\r\\n3. **One-sentence explanation, then \\"That is fixed now.\\"** No mechanics, no system names.\\r\\n4. **Hand them the next step, plus the manual fallback.** The link, \\"pick any time it shows and it will book,\\" and the offer to do it by hand: \\"If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\"\\r\\n5. **Invite the next report:** \\"If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\"\\r\\n6. **Close with \\"Once again, my apologies.\\"** then \\"Best,\\" and \\"Joshua\\".\\r\\n\\r\\nCut from the draft, do not reuse:\\r\\n- \\"Sorry for the runaround\\" as a sign-off. The apology opens the email; it is not a closing quip.\\r\\n- \\"You will get a calendar invite with the Google Meet link right after.\\" Do not narrate what the system will send; it reads as a promise about machinery.\\r\\n- \\"You have all 10 sessions\\" / \\"nothing was used from your package.\\" Leave the package out unless it is needed; the reassurance they want is that nothing is booked, not an accounting.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Situational micro-templates\\r\\n\\r\\nVerbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.\\r\\n  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don\'t worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.\\r\\n- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua\'s explicit go-ahead.\\r\\n- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**\\r\\n  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.\\r\\n- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.\\r\\n  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I\'m glad we got to talk through where you\'re at and what you\'re aiming for on test day.\\r\\n- **Inbound prospect (hasn\'t booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.\\r\\n  > Thanks for reaching out ... I\'d be happy to talk more about what you\'re working on and see if we\'d be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.\\r\\n- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.\\r\\n  > Please don\'t apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The three registers\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich voice governs which surface.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, personal encouragement, email\\r\\n\\r\\nFully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.\\r\\n\\r\\nReference line (target tone):\\r\\n\\r\\n> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let\'s slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You\'ve got this.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it\'s genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.\\r\\n  > Missing this is expected. It\'s one of the hardest moves on the test. Let\'s slow down and see where it slipped.\\r\\n- **Accountability (student isn\'t doing the work):** gentle nudge, low pressure. Frame it as a slip, find a rhythm together. Never \\"the reps aren\'t happening,\\" never tough love.\\r\\n- **Praise (student nails it):** name the exact skill, tie it to their history, raise the bar.\\r\\n  > That\'s the cleanest you\'ve split premise from conclusion yet. Let\'s push it into the harder sets.\\r\\n- **Hard truth about the test:** state it plainly. \\"Do not expect for it to come easy. Expect to find this exhausting.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Teaching content (conversational reasoning) -- lessons, recaps, notes, drills, explanations\\r\\n\\r\\nUse [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md), Joshua\'s selected calibration from 2026-10-02. Keep the reader beside the teacher. Explain the ordinary meaning, ground it in an original example, make the small inferences audible, ask a useful question, and show both the consequence and its limit. Introduce terminology and notation when their meaning is established. Give each concept enough development to be learned from the page.\\r\\n\\r\\nContractions and varied sentence lengths should sound natural when read aloud. Repetition should change the reader\'s view of the same relationship. Questions should give the reader actual thinking work. Light humor may appear within an explanation when it helps understanding; this supersedes the older wit-only-at-the-edges restriction. Do not force punchlines, slang, hyperbole, catchphrases, or paragraph quotas.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source governs delivery, not Joshua\'s procedure, indicator taxonomy, biography, or the scope of a lesson. Preserve his approved methods and logical precision. Personal encouragement still follows the earnest coaching register. Project-owned `lsat-u-voice` routes here and to the detailed standard; an older vendor writer-blend skill must not override this selection.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Brand (sharp wit) -- landing, auth, marketing\\r\\n\\r\\nConfident and clever, the way a good lawyer is clever. Wit tied to the real skill, not wordplay for its own sake. Playful is allowed, but every clever line must be earned and true. Hollow cleverness fails (the killed \\"the next card is the page in front of you\\" was clever but empty).\\r\\n\\r\\n> Most prep teaches the test. We teach the tell.\\r\\n\\r\\nBrand copy speaks as the platform (\\"we\\", \\"you\\"), not as an individual (\\"I\\", \\"Joshua\\"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates the marketing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Don\'t\\r\\n\\r\\n- No cold-testing or gotcha tone. (Ties to teach-first-calibrate-after.)\\r\\n- No condescension or empty cheerleading. Encouragement must sit on real substance.\\r\\n- No generic praise, no hollow cleverness, no hedging (\\"you might want to\\").\\r\\n- No hype/startup vocabulary.\\r\\n- No em dashes, ever.\\r\\n\\r\\nCorrections log: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]] records every student-facing wording fix and\\r\\nthe reason it was wrong. Read it before writing or editing LSAT U copy.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: teach-first-calibrate-after, LSAT U. Source samples: `raw/templates/Golden Email Template Playbook.md`, `raw/templates/email_writing_examples_11_full.txt`, and the full authored-email corpus from the Gmail takeout (944 messages, analyzed 2026-07-18; takeout at `raw/sources/private/takeout/`, git-ignored).\\r\\n---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: LSAT teaching voice standard\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-03\\r\\naliases: [LSAT voice reference, Conditional transcription writing standard]\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, writing]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking\\r\\n\\r\\n**This is Joshua\'s selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the part you need\\r\\n\\r\\n- [The evidence and coverage](#the-evidence-and-coverage)\\r\\n- [The voice in one original passage](#the-voice-in-one-original-passage)\\r\\n- [How the teacher relates to the reader](#how-the-teacher-relates-to-the-reader)\\r\\n- [Sentence length and spoken cadence](#sentence-length-and-spoken-cadence)\\r\\n- [Word choice and useful verbal habits](#word-choice-and-useful-verbal-habits)\\r\\n- [Questions that make the reader think](#questions-that-make-the-reader-think)\\r\\n- [Transitions, repetition, metaphor, and emphasis](#transitions-explain-why-the-next-move-follows)\\r\\n- [The complete source progression and depth](#the-complete-source-progression-and-depth)\\r\\n- [What to carry into the Conditional Bootcamp](#what-to-…1694 tokens truncated…n those roles. Show what the must-arrow records and why English clause order does not determine the two sides.\\r\\n\\r\\n2026-10-03 clarification: explicitly distinguish the indicator from the complete condition it refers to. Sufficient goes left; necessary goes right. The indicator itself is not a condition in the diagram. Show the same relationship with if at the beginning and in the middle, and show necessary targets both before and after requirement language. Neither sentence position nor the words immediately following the indicator determine placement. Preserve if versus only if as different complete expressions.\\r\\n\\r\\nA rule describes what would follow **if** a condition were true; it does not say that condition happened. Bold that “if” and use meaningful in-view motion to expose the connection, with a static reduced-motion equivalent. Replace compressed ready-order wording with a natural full sentence. Give each paragraph and interaction a specific teaching job. These corrections supplement the canonical voice standard rather than adding a new writing register.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Generic reasoning prompts → concrete numbered demonstrations (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua marked four parts of the V6 opening: \\"Read each whole idea,\\" \\"Find what the statements guarantee,\\" the abstract first sentence about information, and the paragraph combining application and linking. They named goals without showing a useful action or explanation. Replace them with numbered, separate scenarios. Show the fact and rule that prove a conclusion about one case; separately show two matching rules and their new conditional conclusion. Labels should identify actual inputs or outputs. Review the whole note for repeated umbrella instructions and make task instructions executable. This follows the teaching voice: specific, concrete, short sentences, with air between distinct ideas.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Fact and rule definitions must explain the inference (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected definitions that remained abstract and an explanation that did not clearly distinguish applying a rule from linking rules. Define the inputs with examples, then show the matching step and exact output. A case fact plus a rule proves something about that case. Two connecting rules produce another conditional rule without establishing a case exists. Adding a fact to the chain is a separate step. Explain all four fact situations and a hidden chain revealed by contraposition rather than leaving them implicit in an interactive model. Rules include general logical relationships, not only policies or obligations.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Boxed `L -> R` → bold teaching notation **L → R** (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected the recap\'s small monospace arrow inside a pale code box. It looked like implementation text and weakened the explanation. Use a real, clearly weighted arrow in ordinary teaching typography. Explain its complete conditions before shortening them. The Some, Most, and All reference also governs the progression: introduce the whole idea, develop the individual components, then compare and combine them. A visual refresh must review that sequence, not only colors and boxes.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Concept lesson\\" → the actual reasoning title (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe recap preview exposed an internal label above the student lesson. Joshua explicitly rejected it. Use a title such as \\"Facts, Rules, and What Follows\\" and headings that teach the reasoning. Keep production labels, version metadata, and design commentary in private records. Check the rendered HTML, since its shell can introduce wording absent from the Markdown source.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Recall Rephrase Colour\\" → \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\"\\r\\nThe door\'s heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line\\r\\nunder a chip reading \\"Read, Recall, Colour\\". \\"Rephrase\\" belongs to a different\\r\\nActivity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why\\r\\n2,600 passing tests never caught it.\\r\\n**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that\\r\\nit matches.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…\\" → plain instruction\\r\\nClinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read \\"with full\\r\\nintonation\\" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation\\r\\ninternal, \\"hear it naturally in your head\\", had been dropped in an earlier edit.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a word like \\"intake\\" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,\\r\\ncheck what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"Three beats through the stimulus\\" → \\"Three steps\\"\\r\\n\\"Beat\\" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary\\r\\nsays students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four\\r\\nthings Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.\\r\\n**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"22 pieces to read\\" → line removed\\r\\nA **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same\\r\\npage use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so \\"22 pieces\\" read as 22\\r\\nsentences.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word\\r\\nalready means on the page before reusing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"say it back\\" → \\"tell it back\\", \\"The Read\\" → \\"The Recall\\"\\r\\nThe library door still carried both retired names. \\"The Read\\" was step four\'s\\r\\nformer name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.\\r\\n**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was\\r\\nstale for months because it is a different database row from the sections.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"then a fourth\\" → \\"then a fourth step\\"\\r\\nThe noun sat three words back and the reader had to carry it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"once you have got all of it\\" → \\"once you have read all of it\\"\\r\\nAmbiguous between having read it and having remembered it, in the one clause\\r\\nthat triggers step four.\\r\\n**Lesson:** precision matters most where the sentence is an instruction.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The three-fact notice → one fact\\r\\n\\"Nothing is recording yet, the microphone opens on Start, and the section\\r\\nrecords in one take that you cannot pause.\\" Two of those are said again in step\\r\\nfour and in the long version, so she skipped the paragraph.\\r\\n**Lesson:** say it once, in the place it matters.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The method, told twice → the long version collapsed\\r\\nThe page rendered four numbered steps and then the whole written briefing saying\\r\\nthe same thing at length, both open. She read the method twice and could not\\r\\ntell which one to follow.\\r\\n**Lesson:** two explanations of one thing is worse than either alone.\\r\\n\\r\\n### \\"PT105.S1 · 24 stimuli · about 25 min\\" → removed\\r\\nThe section was already the second line of the title. The clock is at the foot\\r\\nand in the long version. The stimulus count measured the work in a unit she has\\r\\nno feel for.\\r\\n**Lesson:** delete a line that repeats what is above it. Do not reword it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Booking emails: hand over the link, not the pass settings (2026-09-08)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** \\"70 minutes each, and no expiration date, so there\'s no rush to use them.\\"\\r\\n**Keep:** \\"You\'re set up on LSAT U with 10 sessions.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nSession length and expiry are admin fields. The student sees them on the booking\\r\\npage when she picks a time, so putting them in the email is detail she did not ask\\r\\nfor and has to read past to reach the link. The credit count is worth saying because\\r\\nit tells her how much she has. Everything else about the pass stays in the backend.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** in a booking email, say who it\'s for, how many sessions, and the link.\\r\\nStop there.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Drill hub: say what the button does, not what the feature is called (2026-09-09)\\r\\n\\r\\nSix changes on `/classroom/drill`, from the run-3 pass. The hub named its own\\r\\nfeatures, and it assumed the student had rejected something.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Was | Now |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| \\"Start Smart Drill\\" | \\"Practice your weakest types\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Start One Passage\\" | \\"Read one passage\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Start a Smart Drill instead\\" | \\"Or practice your weakest types\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Read one passage instead\\" | \\"Or read one passage now\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Open analytics\\" | \\"See where you lose points\\" |\\r\\n| \\"Open journal\\" | \\"Review your missed questions\\" |\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Smart Drill\\" is the product\'s internal name for the adaptive set. On a button it\\r\\nasks the student to guess what she is about to get. The sentence above the button\\r\\nstill names it, which is where a product name belongs: after the thing has been\\r\\nexplained, not as the explanation.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Instead\\" was the worse one. It tells the student she has just declined the\\r\\nassigned work by reading past it. She has not. \\"Or\\" offers a second path without\\r\\nnarrating a decision she never made.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Open analytics\\" and \\"Open journal\\" name a screen. A student does not want\\r\\nanalytics, she wants to know where the points are going.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe fallback paragraph lost \\"an adaptive set that follows your weakest question\\r\\ntypes\\" for \\"the question types you miss most\\": the same fact, without the product\\r\\nvocabulary.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** a button says what happens next, in the student\'s words. Feature names\\r\\nbelong in the sentence that explains them, never on the control.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Words never to use in student copy\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Never | Use instead |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| beat, beats | step |\\r\\n| chunk | piece |\\r\\n| intake | plain instruction |\\r\\n| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing\'s own name |\\r\\n| say it back | tell it back |\\r\\n| The Read | The Recall |\\r\\n| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |\\r\\n| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conditional Rephrase: name the student action, not the internal model (2026-09-15)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** “record the route,” “choose the route,” and a map presented as five rules.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** diagram the statement on paper, choose the matching diagram, and organize\\r\\nthe map into four groups. “Required means right” is a separate takeaway because it\\r\\nexplains placement; it is not a fifth group.\\r\\n\\r\\n“Route” described the system’s representation, not the student’s work. Calling five\\r\\nunlike ideas rules also flattened the teaching structure and made the layout harder to\\r\\nread.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** name the action the student can see herself doing. Do not force a teaching\\r\\nreminder into the same hierarchy as the categories it explains.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: [[teaching-voice]] · [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]] · [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session recaps: write a reusable reference, not a play-by-play (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** an opener saying the student got two questions right, followed by a summary of\\r\\nwhich answer they chose and how they explained it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** the concept the student needs tomorrow, a visual model of the reasoning, a\\r\\nshort diagnostic check, and the exact practice they need to complete.\\r\\n\\r\\nA student will not remember an isolated answer-choice exchange a day later, and the\\r\\nexchange is not useful unless it demonstrates a reusable rule. A recap should read like\\r\\na compact study note, not a record of the conversation. Name a question only when the\\r\\ndetails make the concept easier to apply. Praise belongs only when it identifies a\\r\\ndurable skill or meaningful change, not merely that an answer was correct.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** organize the recap around what the student can use next. Lead with the\\r\\nconcept, show the logic clearly, and finish with tangible practice.\\r\\n\\r\\n### A rule is not a fact (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** describing a conditional Must Be True lesson as “applying a rule to a fact”\\r\\nwithout checking whether the stimulus supplied a fact at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** a fact is an asserted true case or condition. A rule is a general conditional\\r\\nrelationship. It tells you what follows if its trigger is present, but it does not say\\r\\nthat the trigger occurred. Some stimuli contain rules only, so the work is to chain\\r\\nthem. When a fact is present, apply a matching rule or a valid contrapositive.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** label the role of each sentence before explaining the inference. Never\\r\\ninvent a fact to make a rule fire.\\r\\n\\r\\n### A post-session note is lecture material, not an email (2026-09-12)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** a greeting, a dated recap heading, references to a question the student is\\r\\nexpected to remember, and a sign-off.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** a concept title, a clear opening distinction, numbered procedures, spaced\\r\\nexamples, and practice that uses the lesson.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** the note should remain useful when the session itself is no longer fresh.\\r\\nIt must teach from the page instead of relying on remembered context.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe concept should not arrive as an abrupt list. Begin with a short explanation of\\r\\nwhy the distinction matters, show the full method at a glance, and then teach each\\r\\npath in the same order. Use tables for comparisons and numbered headings for a\\r\\nprocedure so the student can see the structure before reading the detail.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Define the concept in Joshua\'s language (2026-09-28)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cut:** “Some, most, and all tell you how much of a group fits a description.”\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep:** “Some means at least one. Most means more than half. All means every one,”\\r\\ngrounded in a concrete set of ten cookies before drawing conclusions from the words.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe cut line is vague and does not sound like Joshua teaching. It makes the reader\\r\\ndecode “fits a description” before learning any of the three words. It also risks\\r\\nblurring the difference between a count that is possible and one that is guaranteed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lesson:** open with the actual distinction or definition Joshua taught. Use a\\r\\nconcrete example to show what each statement proves, then introduce notation and\\r\\nthe inferences. Do not write an abstract umbrella sentence merely to announce the\\r\\ntopic.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reference quality does not authorize reference topics (2026-09-30)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua requested the Some, Most, and All note as a teaching-flow and visual-quality reference. Quantity examples and exercises do not belong in the separate facts/rules note. Remove that off-topic material; retain the distinction between what a rule says and what a fact establishes. Before future recaps, read the accumulated corrections in the Post Session Recap skill\'s `references/recap-calibration.md`. Preserve source-driven scope while matching the reference\'s teaching quality.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Conditional practice: explain the decision instead of naming the operation (2026-10-07)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected “same rule, different words,” “equivalent-rule comparison,” “find the requirement, then place it,” “work through the expressions below,” “first choose the complete target,” “read the whole idea,” and “sentence X of Y” when these stood in for a useful explanation or made the drill feel like a collection of labeled sections. “Complete target” and similar terminology should not carry a beginner\'s instructions without a concrete meaning attached.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe revised local copy asks what the learner needs to decide: “Who does the rule say will receive a wristband?”, “What kind of key is needed to open the cabinet?”, and “What kind of pass does entry require?” The lesson demonstrates why before noon, current, registered and with yellow tags matter. Dropping one of those words changes which cases the rule covers. An arrow is checked by imagining its left condition and asking what the sentence then establishes, rather than treating the first English clause as the left side.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua also asked for the mixed drill to train a skill and build a habit, with one question visible and a progress bar. The current local response uses five stages with a concrete purpose for each: retain the full conditions, build a diagram, attempt a translation before supplied labels, compare meanings, then transfer to unfamiliar cases. Error feedback distinguishes dropped qualifications, reversed roles, required versus enough and changed meaning. A fresh question checks the repaired distinction; correcting the exposed answer alone does not demonstrate transfer. Written translations are compared by the learner and are not represented as automatically graded.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep modality in the examples. An electric vehicle that is denied permission disproves the claim that being electric guarantees permission; a vehicle merely staying outside does not. A required action is an obligation, not a fact proving compliance. Confirmation practice for that mistake must test another obligation rather than an unrelated wording comparison.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are demonstrated corrections and the current implementation response, not approval of every exact replacement sentence or proof that the voice matches Joshua identically. The [selected voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) still governs wording, cadence, concrete reasoning and scope. [Current session record](log.md) distinguishes local work from publication.\\r\\n\\r\\nFurther steering in the same session: “Keep the complete condition” was itself awkward as a visible heading. Ask who or what the particular rule covers instead. Joshua also rejected the landing page\'s total question/stage counts, per-stage counts, long stage list and padded explanation of repairs. A shorter launch should explain the immediate task; each stage should introduce its purpose and instructions when the learner reaches it. That latest flow revision was requested while final verification was still pending, so this record does not certify its implementation.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### RC actions should describe what the student gets (2026-10-07)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua rejected Inspect set as unclear and awkward. Use Preview drill for viewing the exact selected passages and questions before starting. Keep Start this drill distinct and explain whether answers are hidden and whether work is saved. Generic Menu is also insufficient where the actual action is Question details. This correction does not justify adding more launch instructions, repeated summaries, or unrelated assigned work inside RC setup.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}}\n{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"7e3c19","wall_time_seconds":0.7294936,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":335,"output":"\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\styles.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\openingCoursePath.ts\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\notes.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\content\\\\foundations.source.ts\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\sufficient-condition-experience.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\reference-sequence.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningFoundations.test.tsx\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningCourseContent.tsx\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningCourseContent.test.tsx\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningCourse.tsx\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningCourse.test.tsx\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\opening.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\opening-polish.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\opening-mixed-drill.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\opening-course.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\opening-activity-practice.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\necessary-condition-tabs.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\must-arrow-practice.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\must-arrow-note.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\lesson-pages.css\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\ConditionalReferenceSequence.tsx\\r\\nsrc/bootcamps/conditionals\\\\components\\\\ConditionalReferenceSequence.test.tsx\\r\\n\\r\\n"}}'}]

Now look at the relationship from the other side. You can\'t be enrolled in law school without being a student. Being a student is the <strong>necessary condition</strong>, the part that must be true. That\'s what we\'ll examine next.</p></aside>\\r\\n265-      </div>\\n266-      <p>Next, we\'ll examine what the sufficient condition requires.</p>\\n267-    </ReferenceStep>\\n268-\\r\\n269-    <ReferenceStep number={3} label=\\"The sufficient family\\" title=\\"Different words can preserve the same rule\\">\\r\\n--\\n271-      <p>For the next examples, use a different stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. We will express that same connection in several ways. We haven\'t said everyone with a key borrows a locker.</p>\\r\\n272-      <WordFamily entries={sufficientFamily} role=\\"sufficient\\"/>\\r\\n273-      <p>When/whenever and any/anytime group related forms together. An ending changes the kind of case being discussed, but these examples keep the same connection. For example, every guest wearing a blue badge covers that whole group. Guest alone would include people without badges. Use the expression to find the group, then keep the words that limit it.</p>\\r\\n274:      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Every guest wearing a blue badge receives a lunch voucher. Which complete idea is enough?\\" options={[\'Guest\', \'Guest wearing a blue badge\', \'Receives a lunch voucher\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Guest alone drops the badge restriction and would make a claim about unbadged guests.\', \'The rule gives a voucher to every guest wearing a blue badge. Keep that qualification with the person.\', \'The voucher is what the rule establishes. It has not said every voucher recipient wears a blue badge.\']}/>\\r\\n275-      <details className=\\"cr-extension\\"><summary>Two more Group 1 expressions</summary><p><strong>Where</strong> can state a relationship across locations: “Where a collection point operates, parcels can be returned.” An operating collection point establishes the availability of returns there. Availability is not proof that someone made a return.</p><p><strong>The only</strong> needs its complete phrase: “The only notebooks on this shelf are hardback.” A notebook on the shelf is hardback. The sentence does not put every hardback notebook there.</p><p>These are part of your Group 1 work. Understand what the full expression covers before using its group as a placement shortcut.</p></details>\\r\\n276-      <p>We have seen what can be enough. Now let\'s name what that condition requires.</p>\\r\\n277-    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n278-\\r\\n279-    <ReferenceStep number={4} label=\\"The necessary condition\\" title=\\"Necessary means required\\">\\r\\n280-      <div className=\\"cr-sufficiency nc-lesson\\">\\r\\n281-        <p className=\\"cr-sufficiency-lead\\"><strong>A necessary condition is a requirement. [index] : []);\n+\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    if (!focusOnArrival) return;\n+    heading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true });\n+    heading.current?.scrollIntoView?.({ block: 'nearest', behavior: 'instant' });\n+    setFocusOnArrival(false);\n+  }, [current, finished, focusOnArrival]);\n+\n+  function updateAnswer(update: Partial<Answer>) {\n+    setAnswers(previous => previous.map((item, index) => index === current ? { ...item, ...update } : item));\n+  }\n+  function go(index: number) { setCurrent(index); setFinished(false); setFocusOnArrival(true); }\n+  function checkAnswer() {\n+    if (answer.selected === null) return;\n+    updateAnswer({ checked: true, first: answer.first ?? answer.selected });\n+  }\n+  function tryAgain() {\n+    updateAnswer({ selected: null, checked: false });\n+    firstOption.current?.focus();\n+  }\n+\n+  return <section className=\"d1-experience\" aria-label=\"Drill 1 questions\">\n+    <div className=\"d1-progress-header\"><span className=\"d1-eyebrow\">{finished ? firstOption : undefined} type=\"button\" key={option} className=\"d1-option\" aria-pressed={selected} disabled={answer.checked} data-selected={selected} data-correct={answer.checked && index === question.correct} data-incorrect={answer.checked && selected && index !== question.correct} onClick={() => updateAnswer({ selected: index })}><span className=\"d1-option-letter\" aria-hidden=\"true\">{answer.checked && index === question.correct ? <Check size={17}/> : String.fromCharCode(65 + index)}</span><span>{option}</span>{answer.checked && index === question.correct && <span className=\"d1-sr-only\">Correct answer</span>}</button>;\n+        })}</div>\n+        <div className=\"d1-feedback-region\" role=\"status\" aria-live=\"polite\">{answer.checked && answer.selected !== null && <div className=\"d1-feedback\" data-correct={answer.selected === question.correct}><div className=\"d1-feedback-title\"><span className=\"d1-feedback-dot\"/>{answer.selected === question.correct ?
